Reconstruction record

The analytic chapters follow a possible zero from its residue to the one-sided window estimate and reflection. The theta chapters reconstruct an integral expansion at the selected primes, the local zeta quotients and the continued norm formula. The arithmetic chapter derives the marked-window selection, degree-two estimate and separate character counts. Each chapter records its remaining inputs. The chapter on normalization follows the six prime classes and the nonzero base tuple. The arithmetic chapter constructs the finite cycles and their determinant evaluations. Further inputs include integral gluing of lattices, representation densities and the estimates for quadratic twists. An end-to-end review of the exposition is still in progress. Current project state

  6. LOC-04The price of a mixed jet

    For the finite multi-index κ≤a coordinatewise and the valued-field, integral-series, BHyp, weight and substitution hypotheses in LemmaRA, the factored jet has normalized valuation at least ∑κᵢ(γₚ−σᵢ)−|ι|(logₚ a+1). Each offending pure pair below κ requires p^ασᵢ+p^βσⱼ≥1.

    mathematical implication reconstructed under its stated hypotheses
    Declaration and correspondence

    The proof raises the radii, bounds the integral substitution and exponential separately, then multiplies by the multifactorial. The full BHyp realization is open in this exposition.

  10. CLS-02Reflection of a character zero

    For a primitive ψ of nonzero modulus and a zero with 0<Re s<1, L(ψ⁻¹,1−s)=0. For arbitrary χ the primitive transfer and right-half-plane nonvanishing imply Re s=½.

    mathematical implication reconstructed under its stated hypotheses
    Declaration and correspondence

    Parity gives a nonvanishing gamma factor. The completed equation uses the inverse character.

  12. CIM-01Removing an inert ideal factor while preserving the period lattice

    For each p≥5 inert in the CM quadratic field, nonzero curve ideal I of norm prime to 2d, discriminant root δ and embedding of the algebraic numbers into ℂ_p, the theorem may replace I by I′ of norm prime to p. Writing I=(p)^k I′, δ′=p^kδ gives the identical complex period lattice and the prescribed twelfth root for I′.

    independently reviewed
    Declaration and correspondence

    The global theta host requires an inert-class or signal prime. It obtains the genus-one model at this fixed embedding before choosing orthonormal bases. Entry estimates use a further level-free hypothesis. The local example starts with I=(5) and ends with I′=𝒪_K in K=ℚ(√−23).

    Review. Two independent readers compared the actual host and lattice suppliers and checked the direction δ′=p^kδ, the discriminant scaling, inertness of 5, and equality of the two period lattices.

    Used by. CIM-02

  13. CIM-02Constructing unramified integral Weierstrass coefficients from j

    The chosen CM2 supplier places integral J in the completed unramified field by inertia invariance. For J≠0,1728 write v(J)=3a and v(J−1728)=2b. Then t=p^(b−a), C=27J/(J−1728) give G₂=t²C and G₃=t³C of valuations a and b and unit discriminant. These are invariants of a scaled copy of the original curve; the bounded coefficients of the short equation lie in the unramified integer ring.

    independently reviewed
    Declaration and correspondence

    The separating polynomial and an Artin-trivial Frobenius at a degree-one prime yield an inertia-fixed CM j-invariant. Divisibilities of valuations are separate CM inputs. The cases J=0 and 1728 use (0,1) and (1,0). The scale is a scalar in ℂ_p; its norm is one for the discriminant-normalized period lattice.

    Review. Two independent readers checked the valuation sign for t, both invariant valuations, the zero valuation of t⁶C²(C−27), the exceptional pairs, and the distinction between the coefficient field and the field of the scaling isomorphism.

    Formulas in the book. cm inert good eq

  14. CIM-03The integral height-two parameter and its inverse

    At an inert prime p≥5 the selected CM3 theorem yields a unit ε in the completed unramified field for which Θ=e_E(εℓ) has integral coefficients, where e_E is the curve formal exponential and ℓ=Σ T^(p^(2j))/p^j. The inverse of Θ is defined over the same integer ring and satisfies ℓ∘Θ⁻¹=ε⁻¹L_E.

    independently reviewed
    Declaration and correspondence

    The CM generator is exchanged with its conjugate by Frobenius and has unit difference. The multiplication-by-p level bound, height-two functional equation, and the solution ε=cσ²ε put the logarithms in matching form. The use of the integral coordinate in the theta comparison is a separate supplied theorem.

    Review. Two independent readers checked ε=cσ²ε and the inverse-coordinate identity. The example in 𝔽₂₅ gives ω⁵=1−ω, with initial logarithm terms T+T²⁵/5+T⁶²⁵/25.

  15. CNT-01The two auxiliary-prime level indices

    At q, the ratio of row and column indices is q^[mk+m(m−1)/2] times the product of (1−q^(−j)) over k<j≤r, r=k+m−1. Its f repetitions at each of g₀,g₁ yield the normalized leading term [m+m(m−1)/(2k)]log(g₀g₁). The remaining contribution is between −2log(2)/k and zero.

    mathematical proof reconstructed
    Declaration and correspondence

    Count GL by independent columns and divide by each subgroup. With s=m/2−1 the formal row count s(2k+1+2s)+k(k−1)/2 equals r(r−1)/2−k. Splitting Π_r/Π_k cancels the first k factors. LinkMin weakens log(1/2) to −1 per prime. Small example: 6/2=3, 168/2=84 and their ratio is 28.

    Hypotheses. Finite prime fields, k≥1, even m≥2, exactly two type primes, and f>0. At the worked local example k=m=q=2 there is no claim of full family admissibility. The family has m≥44 eventually.

    Review. Independent reviewer checked the free-entry pattern and index exponents, the factor 7/8 and model ratio 28, and the two-prime logarithmic bounds.

    Formulas in the book. count five ratio · count five normalized

  16. CNT-02The index term in the mass comparison for a word

    X_u^(fk)=Dstar/dstar_u equals the positive mass ratio times the computed index ratio times the product over places of the formal degree divided by the column Weyl dimension. Successive mass factors change the factorial product from rank k to rank r. In the large family the sharp log X_u floor is S + m log(g₀g₁) − 11L; the prime window then costs at most 2 on the log scale at its lower end.

    identity and quantitative index contribution reconstructed
    Declaration and correspondence

    Separating mass, local indices and placewise dimensions leaves a triangular sum of factorial logarithms. The local index contribution is the calculation in this record. MSF-01 and MSF-02 derive the complete −11L allowance, and JointsL uses the upper and lower components of TorusPairsWithVariant, independently of its extra bound on the rank ratio.

    Hypotheses. Base volume equals genus mass at r; the positive Siegel mass ratio holds for the consecutive ranks; positive column dimensions and formal degrees. The sharp floor invokes DataA including separated weights, 44≤m≤k, π≤v, and scale/gap bounds. Γ>1 and log Γ/(fk)≤1 at the actual family point. The family threshold is chosen for fixed ε,C₀,C_Y before x,i,u.

    Review. Independent reviewer confirmed the f repetitions in the mass identity and the uniform Γ correction in the window.

  17. CYC-01A closed cyclic word in degree twice the twist

    In the bar chains of C=ℤ/q with ℤ/q coefficients, the sum of words [j₁|1|…|jₑ|1] over all e-tuples is a cycle in degree 2e.

    independently reviewed
    Declaration and correspondence

    The dropped initial entry sums to q copies of a word. Successive contractions agree after shifting one or two summation indices and have opposite signs.

    Hypotheses. q>0 and e≥1. The application has q=ℓ^ν. Each group entry is written additively. Lean uses zc q (e−1).

    Review. A separate reader checked the actual source, the degree and sign conventions, the scope of the homology generator count and the matrix transfer, and independently evaluated both twists of the example.

  18. CYC-02A homology relation with an invertible coefficient

    For the maps dₜ(a)=(a,ta) into C², some ℤ/q linear combination of the 2e+2 images of the cyclic word is a boundary with a unit among its coefficients.

    independently reviewed
    Declaration and correspondence

    Two short exact sequences from the cyclic difference map give at most 2e+1 generators in degree 2e. Lifting the classes to a free abelian group permits cancelling the common prime factors before projection. After projection to homology the vanishing combination is represented by a boundary in the bar complex.

    Hypotheses. As in CYC-01 with q a power of the prime ℓ, t=0,…,2e+1. The generator bound applies to homology with trivial coefficients, as an abelian group.

    Review. A separate reader checked the actual source, the degree and sign conventions, the scope of the homology generator count and the matrix transfer, and independently evaluated both twists of the example.

  19. CYC-03The scalar residue functional and its prism value

    On k× the degree-(2e+1) cocycle built from e logarithmic carries and v takes the prism of the weight-x cyclic word and g to xᵉv(g) in ℤ/q.

    independently reviewed
    Declaration and correspondence

    The logarithm has a prescribed residue at ζ and its integer carry satisfies the cocycle identity. Cyclic shifts telescope to x. Symmetric carries cancel neighboring prism insertions, leaving e independent factors and the index of the gluing element.

    Hypotheses. k a finite field, ζ∈k× of order q>0, e≥1, x a natural number, v:k×→ℤ/q an additive index and g∈k×. The construction works for any such homomorphism v.

    Review. A separate reader checked the actual source, the degree and sign conventions, the scope of the homology generator count and the matrix transfer, and independently evaluated both twists of the example.

  20. CYC-04The projective-line transfer reads the determinant

    For a finite k-vector space V and a gluing automorphism g on a pure scalar weight-x block, the transferred functional evaluates its prism to xᵉv(det g) in ℤ/q.

    independently reviewed
    Declaration and correspondence

    Line ratios along successive inverse actions define a transfer commuting with boundaries. The two projective fibers in V⊕W have cardinalities |W| and 1, equal modulo q. The prism law gives a homomorphism, and elementary shears have trivial image. Direct sums and the scalar case then determine its value on a diagonal and hence on every invertible matrix.

    Hypotheses. Under the assumptions of CYC-03. The finite field has |k|≡1 modulo q because ζ has order q. The source separately handles q=1.

    Review. A separate reader checked the actual source, the degree and sign conventions, the scope of the homology generator count and the matrix transfer, and independently evaluated both twists of the example.

  21. CYE-01The signed divisor of a Jacobi product

    For a degree-one prime P above p∤N in K_N, J=∏_{1≤j<b}Jac(χ,χ^j) gives X_P=c(J)/J with exponent θ_N(t) at σ_t⁻¹P. The sum controlling membership vanishes exactly when the residues add without a carry.

    mathematical proof reconstructed
    Declaration and correspondence

    The no-carry count e(t) is the exponent of P_t in J. Conjugation exchanges t and −t, so c(J)/J has exponent e(−t)−e(t). Finite-field power sums justify the criterion; the example uses p=11,N=5,b=3.

    Hypotheses. N≥1, 0≤b<N, p prime, reduction O_K→F_p and lifted χ on F_p× as in xJac. For each factor 0<1,j,1+j<N. At b=0 or 1 the product is empty.

    Review. A second reader confirmed the inverse orientation, the signed subtraction, the special case b=0 and the exponents of the numerical example.

  22. CYE-02Principalization for every ideal class and its ambiguity

    For every nonzero integral ideal 𝔞 of K_N there is X with (X)=∏(σ_t⁻¹𝔞)^θ_N(t) and Xc(X)=1. For any two such solutions X′=wX with w^(2N)=1.

    mathematical proof reconstructed
    Declaration and correspondence

    A solution for (y)=𝔞P is the product of its inverse conjugates with floor exponents. Divide by X_P. Equal principal ideals make the quotient of two solutions a unit; its conjugate absolute values impose cyclotomic torsion.

    Hypotheses. N≥1 and 0≤b<N for existence. The uniqueness statement applies for any b and two solutions for the same nonzero ideal. The chosen auxiliary degree-one prime represents [𝔞]⁻¹ outside primes dividing N.

    Review. A second reader confirmed the inverse-class choice, division order and torsion exponent against xEx, xMul and xUniq.

    Uses in this exposition. CYE-01

    Used by. CYE-03 · CYE-04

  30. HOM-01Torsion in an integral lattice presentation

    If the rank r of the ambient free abelian group is at most A and each integral relation has ℓ¹ norm at most b, the cokernel has finite torsion of order at most b^A.

    mathematical proof reconstructed

    Bound each determinant by the product of row sums via absolute values of the permutation expansion, followed by summation over all row choices. The argument uses integral coordinates in that completed sublattice and rational independence of the added directions.

    Declaration and correspondence

    Declaration and correspondence

    For an odd prime ℓ and level F=f_{ℓ′}ℓ^m, k=e+1≥2 and a primitive character of the selected parity, let χ be its induced character at F. The signed-carry sum S satisfies D[χ(b)b^e S−2(χ(b)b^k−1)T_F]≡0 modulo ℓ^m, where T_F is the generalized Bernoulli value at F divided by k. When χ(b)b^k−1 is a unit and m meets the strict depth condition, the lifted scalar z has the same ℓ-adic norm as the primitive Bernoulli value divided by k.

    independently reviewed

    On the CD Haar measure the formal degree of a mixed-signature large-gap discrete series is V(λ)/F_N, where F_N=0!1!⋯(N−1)!. Translation makes d/V constant on an admissible ray. The lowest-compact-type Weyl formula and a Gaussian coefficient limit determine that constant.

    mathematical proof reconstructed
    Declaration and correspondence

    The sign counts p,q give pq complex coordinates. FdStep uses nested admissible coordinate shifts. ThmFD bounds the same extended-real limsup and liminf by cPQ·PiW(0). FdConst expands the ordinary sequence via lemma7, then identifies its positive real limit. At ordering i<j each opposite-sign weight at zero is j−i.

  55. HOB-02Residue counting for a bounded determinant norm

    For any number field F and an integral N-by-N frame A with r=|Norm det A|>0, B=rA⁻¹ is integral. Equal residues of B mod r for frames of the same r imply left GL_N(O_F)-equivalence. At most r^{dN} representatives suffice at norm r, and J^{1+dN} suffice for 1≤r≤J.

    statement and proof inspected
    Declaration and correspondence

    Norm membership in the determinant ideal clears denominators. A' A⁻¹=1+A'X and its inverse 1−AX are integral when B−B'=rX. Equal residues are a sufficient condition; choosing a representative of each realized residue covers all orbits.

  77. MSF-01The normalized local formal degree at one place

    For every word u and every infinite place ρ, log(dρ F_r/V(u)) ≥ (k−1)∑_j log b_j + log(2/3) − 5kL. Consequently, subtracting k∑_j log w_jρ leaves at least −7kL−k−1.

    mathematical proof reconstructed
    Declaration and correspondence

    The row's cross-block factors give ∏ b_j^(k−1), its negative-negative gaps cost nothing. Strip the 2k−3 endpoint factors from V(u); the remaining gaps inject into nonadjacent c gaps, and every unused c gap is at least one. Divide and take logs; the dimension comparison supplies 2/3, the place estimate budgets 5kL, and replacing (k−1)log b by k log w over the m terms costs ≤k+2kL. The algebra example has V(4,0,−3,−5)=7560 and V(6,2,−1)=84.

    Hypotheses. At an admissible DataA family point in the eventual large-gap range, ε>0, L=x^ε≥1, r=k+m−1, m+3≤k, 44≤m≤min(x,k), Δ≥1, 2(log 8+1+log x)≤L, log(2k)≤L. Row and column parameters interlace; c_i≥0, and adjacent c and b gaps are at least one. The formal degree is taken in the prescribed normalization. Its gap condition uses signature (P,Q), tolerance η=1/3 with exp(−r)≤η<1/2, and opposite-sign gaps at least 8192 min(P,Q)(PQ)^3 log(r)/η².

    Review. Independent reader checked the example (ratio 90), 2/3, 5kL, k+2kL and the need for m/x≤1; its small ranks only model the interlacing comparison.

  78. MSF-02The Euler and discriminant costs in the lower mass bound

    log X_u ≥ S+m log(g₀g₁)−11L, where S=f⁻¹∑_ρ∑_j log(w_jρ v/π). Before division by k, a sufficient budget is 8kL+(m−1)L/2+m+2+k≤11kL.

    mathematical proof reconstructed
    Declaration and correspondence

    At prime p the absolute logarithmic weight of ideal P is bounded by (f(P/p)/2)[−log(1−p^(−M))]. The sum of those weights is controlled by f and the Euler product for ζ(M). Thus after averaging, r−k=m−1 Euler terms cost at most m−1. Discard the nonnegative m(m−1)/2 surpluses on log(v/π) and log(g₀g₁); the remaining discriminant cost is kL+(m−1)L/2 and the two primes cost ≤2. Add the local cost 7kL+k+1 and divide by k.

    Hypotheses. For fixed FamP(ε) with ε>0 and fixed C₀,C_Y there is x₀ uniform in the allowed x≥x₀, index i, every Δ,w satisfying DataA and every word. Positive mass ratio and matching base volume, unramified CM field of degree 2f, f≥1, π≤v, log(2v)≤L and the local/size hypotheses of MSF-01. The type-prime products are indexed by exactly two primes and the mass Euler terms by k<M≤r.

    Review. Independent reader checked the f normalization, m−1 Euler increments, the two-prime remainder, the signs of the discarded terms and the bound 11; the arithmetic rearrangement agrees with log_X_lower_arithmetic.

    For a large host, odd ℓ and 1≤e<t, use the characters of the quotient that have parity (−1)^e. The exterior reduction is lossless when ℓ does not divide the subgroup index.   117. PRP-02A uniform exception bound from zero density and mean square

    For suitable prime progression P in (√U,U] with |P|≥U^(9/10) and ≤R primitive base characters of conductor ≤F≤√U, the primes failing at some twist or height in [Y,Y^K] occupy ≤η|P| past a common threshold.

    mathematical proof reconstructed
    Declaration and correspondence

    A prime failure injects into a bad primitive character since fp=f′p′ and f′≤F<p forces p=p′. The ratio of the zero-density exception count to the population is O(U^(−21/40)). For a witness height depending on p, control the derivative by the same quadratic mean square with coefficients restricted to n>y. Cauchy–Schwarz and integration give 4CV log(2V)|P| on each dyadic block. Markov and summation give (32C/τ)Y^(−τ)/(1−2^(−τ)). Sum the latter over ≤R characters with allocation η/(2(R+1)); add the η/2 zero-density allowance. Outside both sets the explicit formula estimates the centered sum.

    Hypotheses. General stage: 0<ε≤1/4, τ>0, K≥1, η>0, positive modulus divisible by 8, primitive reduced class, log-modulus and upper and lower height limits of admitted; each prime divisor of the modulus is ≤Y^(ε/5) or >(Y^K)^400.

  157. SEL-01Why a unit determinant pays for q to the complex rank

    Let C be the ideal class group modulo δ=(γ⁻¹−a) and E the integral unit group modulo the image of δ and all roots of unity. |EqHom|≤|C|ℓ, |W|=|E||C| and |E|(a−1)=(a^n−1)^r₂. Consequently |EqHom| q^r₂ ≤ |W|ℓ ℓ^(c+f), with f=vℓ(e).

    mathematical proof reconstructed
    Declaration and correspondence

    A character's extension to the divisor group kills principal divisors and δ of any divisor. Evaluate on singleton primes to recover the original function. For finite class group the kernel and cokernel of δ have equal orders. The modulus-free determinant calculation adjoins a trivial ℤ-line to log units, giving r₂ full cycles of length n and an auxiliary (a−1) factor on the constant line. Applying LTE gives vℓ(|E|)+c+f=(ν+f)r₂, and weakening (ν+f)r₂≥νr₂ accounts for the factor in the stated inequality.

    Hypotheses. Totally complex fixed base field L′; γ generates Gal(F/L′) and acts on a primitive ℓ^ν-th root by 1+ℓ^c; n=ord γ=ℓ^(ν−c), 1≤c≤ν, c≥2 when ℓ=2, a=(1+ℓ^c)^e with e≥1, q=ℓ^ν, r₂ the number of complex places of L′. All divisor and class groups here belong to the upper field F.

    Review. Checked the gamma inverse on divisors and units, finite character bound, both valuations and the determinant factor from the extra line. Small example at ℓ=3,c=2,e=3,ν=3 has v₃(1000−1)=3 and v₃(1000³−1)=4.

    |EqHom|q^r₂ ≤ B ℓ^X with X=(b+f)g+c+f, g=2[L′:ℚ]+⌊log₂ h(L′)⌋+2. For the detecting-cycle count B=q^r₂ T_(2e+1)T_(2e), cancel q^r₂ to obtain a bound on |EqHom| uniform in ν.

    mathematical proof reconstructed
    Declaration and correspondence

    mathematical proof reconstructed
    Declaration and correspondence

  163. SIE-02The Siegel increment from two limits and transfer to the indefinite row

    For the chosen standard orthonormal lattices, the quotient mass_M/mass_(M−1) is {[(M−1)!(v/π)^M]^f/√((2v)^f)} Lη(M) for M≥3. The positive rank-one mass normalization cancels. The same normalized ratio for unit-vector complements propagates the definite rank-two identity to all constant signatures (P,Q) with P≥2 and hence to Y₀, whose dimension is r; the Count5 scale uses exactly k<M≤r.

    mathematical proof reconstructed
    Declaration and correspondence

    Normalize the weighted ball count at radius j by j^(2fM). Grouping nonzero vectors of the same length yields the representation mass sum, together with the zero-vector term. The lattice limit is μΛ(π^M/M!)^f/(v^(fM)δ). The local density limit is c_(M−1)λΛ M^(−f)Lη(M)/(δ√((2v)^f)). Cancel the nonzero δ and M^f in (M!)^f, obtaining the stated factorial and ratio. For an orthonormal lattice the λΛ-factors are all one. In the volume induction the complement of a norm +1 vector decreases P at Q=0, while a norm −1 vector decreases Q at fixed P≥2. For (P,Q)=sigY₀ their sum is r. The k-to-r telescope has m−1 increments.

    Hypotheses. E is an unramified CM field with fixed real field in the sense IsCMUnram, f=#infinite places=[E:ℚ]/2, v=|disc E|^(1/(2f))/2. In the mass recursion the Hermitian lattice is integral, totally positive definite, and spans a finite-dimensional E-space. The rank increment comparing orthonormal models is used at M≥3. The representation estimate invokes the binary Hermitian representation result and the pinned local density identity. For the indefinite transfer P≥2, the volume/measure tie and a signed norm-one orthogonal-complement volume formula hold. Thresholds and constants in other Count5 estimates are unchanged by this pointwise identity.

    Review. An independent reader checked both limiting coefficients, signs of the two inductions, f exponents and (M−1)! normalization. At M=3 and a finite prime of the real subfield with residue cardinality 2 the local Euler contribution is 8/9 in the inert case and 8/7 for a split pair. The two alternatives do not yield a numerical global mass.

    mathematical proof reconstructed

  180. INP-17The sign and power in the layer cokernel

    For e≠0 and a degree-ℓ^m cyclotomic layer, the locally trivial degree-one length equals the cokernel length of (χ(γ)^e x₀(γ))^(ℓ^m)−1. Degrees and twists refer to a common tower generator.
